“But the outstanding and most significant quality was his profound criminality. Premeditated murder is the most atrocious of crimes, and murder for expediency is the most atrocious form of murder. This man, then, was of a profoundly criminal type, and was most probably a practicing criminal.
===Chinese===
“然而他最触目、最要紧的品质，是那份根深蒂固的恶性。预谋杀人已是众罪之极，而为权宜杀人又是凶杀中的极恶。由此可见，此人属于罪恶至深的一类，而且十有八九是个职业惯犯。

===Original===
“Turning now to the victim, the evidence showed that he was a man of high moral qualities: honest, industrious, thrifty, kindly and amiable, and of good reputation﻿—the exact reverse of the other. Any illicit association between these two men was, therefore, excluded, and yet there must have been an association of some kind. Of what kind could it have been?
===Chinese===
“再看被害人。证据表明，他是个道德品质极高的人：诚实、勤勉、节俭、和善可亲，名声清白——与那一个恰成正反。因此，这两人之间有什么非法勾连，是可以排除的；然而他们之间必定有过某种关联。那能是一种什么关联呢？

===Original===
“Now, in the case of this man, as in that of the other, there was one outstanding fact. He was a sculptor. And not only a sculptor, but an artist in the highest class of waxwork. And not only this. He was probably the only artist of this kind practicing in this country. For waxwork is almost exclusively a French art. So far as I know, all the wax figures and high-class lay figures that are made are produced in France. This man, therefore, appeared to be the unique English practitioner of this very curious art.
===Chinese===
“在这一个人身上，同在那一个身上一样，有一个突出的事实：他是雕塑家。而且不止是雕塑家，还是最高品级的蜡像艺术家。还不止于此。他多半是本国从事这门手艺的独一位艺术家。因为蜡像几乎是一门专属法国的艺术。据我所知，凡有出产的蜡像和高档人形模特（lay figure），无一不是法国制造。因此，这个人看来便是这门极为奇特的技艺在英国的仅存传人。

===Original===
“The fact impressed me profoundly. To realize its significance we must realize the unique character of the art. Waxwork is a fine art; but it differs from all other fine arts in that its main purpose is one that is expressly rejected by all those other arts. An ordinary work of sculpture, no matter how realistic, is frankly an object of metal, stone, or pottery. Its realism is restricted to truth of form. No deception is aimed at but, on the contrary, is expressly avoided. But the aim of waxwork is complete deception; and its perfection is measured by the completeness of the deception achieved. How complete that may be can be judged by incidents that have occurred at Madame Tussaud’s. When that exhibition was at the old Baker Street Bazaar, the snuff-taker﻿—whose arms, head, and eyes were moved by clockwork﻿—used to be seated on an open bench; and it is recorded that, quite frequently, visitors would sit down by him on the bench and try to open conversation with him. So, too, the waxwork policeman near the outer door was occasionally accosted with questions by arriving visitors.
===Chinese===
“这个事实给了我极深的印象。要领会它的分量，先得领会这门艺术的独特品性。蜡像是一门美术；但它有别于其他一切美术之处在于：它的主要目的，恰是其他那些美术明确弃绝的东西。一件寻常的雕塑，无论何等写实，坦率地说就是一件金属、石头或陶土的物件。它的写实只限于形态之真。它不以骗人为鹄的，反而刻意避而不为。而蜡像的鹄的却是彻头彻尾的欺骗；它的完美，以所达成的欺骗之完全程度来衡量。这完全能达到什么地步，杜莎夫人蜡像馆里出过的事便是明证。当年那展览还设在老贝克街市集（Baker Street Bazaar）的时候，那位取鼻烟的蜡人——他的手臂、头颅和眼睛都由钟表机关驱动——惯常坐在一条四面敞开的长凳上；据记载，常有看客在他身旁坐下，想同他搭话。同样，外门近旁那个蜡像警察，也时不时被进门的看客拦住问话。

===Original===
“Bearing this fact in mind, it is obvious that this art is peculiarly adapted to employment in certain kinds of fraud, such as personation, false alibi, and the like; and it is probable that the only reason why it is not so employed is the great difficulty of obtaining first-class waxworks.
===Chinese===
“把这个事实记在心里，便一目了然：这门技艺特别宜于用在某些诈骗上头，比如冒名顶替、伪造不在场证明之类；它之所以未见有人这么用，恐怕唯一的原因就是头等蜡像极难得手。

===Original===
“Naturally, then, when I observed this connection of a criminal with a waxwork artist, I asked myself whether the motive of the murder was not to be sought in that artist’s unique powers. Could it be that an attempt had been made to employ the deceased on some work designed for a fraudulent purpose? If such an attempt had been made, whether it had or had not been successful, the deceased would be in possession of knowledge which would be highly dangerous to the criminal; but especially if a work had actually been executed and used as an instrument of fraud.
===Chinese===
“于是自然而然，当我留意到一个罪犯与一位蜡像艺术家之间的这种关联，我便自问：谋杀的动机，是否正应到那位艺术家的独门本领中去寻找。会不会是有人曾试图雇死者制作某件意在诈骗的作品？倘若有过这样的企图，那么不论成没成，死者都已掌握了足以置罪犯于死地的内情；而若作品当真做了出来，又确实被用作诈骗的工具，那就更不必说了。

===Original===
“But there were other possibilities in the case of a sculptor who was also a medallist. He might have been employed to produce﻿—quite innocently﻿—copies of valuable works which were intended for fraudulent use; and the second stage of the investigation was concerned with these possibilities. That stage was ushered in by Follett’s discovery of the guinea; the additional facts that we obtained at the Museum, and later, when we learned that the guinea that had been found was an electrotype copy, and that deceased was an expert electrotyper, all seemed to point to the production of forgeries as the crime in which Julius D’Arblay had been implicated. That was the view to which we seemed to be committed; but it did not seem to me satisfactory, for several reasons. First, the motive was insufficient﻿—there was really nothing to conceal. When the forgeries were offered for sale, it would be obvious that someone had made them, and that someone could be traced by the purchaser through the vendor. The killing of the actual maker would give no security to the man who sold the forgeries, and who would have to appear in the transaction. And then, although deceased was unique as a waxworker, he was not as a copyist or electrotyper. For those purposes, much more suitable accomplices might have been found. The execution of copies by deceased appeared to be a fact; but my own feeling was that they had been a mere byproduct﻿—that they had been used as a means of introduction to deceased for some other purpose connected with waxwork.
===Chinese===
“不过，对一位兼为章牌师的雕塑家来说，还有别的可能。他也许受人雇用——全然无辜地——复制过一些贵重作品，而那些复制品是打算派诈骗用场的；调查的第二阶段便是围绕这些可能性展开的。那个阶段由福利特探长发现那枚几尼开了头；随后我们在大英博物馆查得的种种实情，加上后来获悉所发现的那枚几尼是件电铸复制品、死者又是精通此道的电铸匠——这一切似乎都指向伪币制造，即尤利乌斯·达布雷与之有涉的那桩罪行。我们似乎已被逼上了这个看法；可我总觉得它不能令人满意，缘由有好几条。其一，动机不充分——实在没有什么要隐瞒的。伪币拿到市面上售卖时，明摆着总有人造了它们，而买主顺藤摸瓜，从卖主就能寻到制作者。杀掉实际的制作者，对那个售卖伪币、必须在交易里露面的人毫无保障可言。再者，死者作为蜡像艺人是独一无二的，作为复制匠或电铸匠却未必。为这类用途，本可以找到合适得多的同伙。死者确实复制过作品，这看来是实情；但我自己的感觉是，那些复制品不过是一项副产品——它们被用作结识死者的一个引子，为的是另一件与蜡像有干的事。

===Original===
“At the end of this stage we had made some progress. We had identified this unknown man with another unknown man, who was undoubtedly a professional criminal. We had found, in the forged guinea, a possible motive for the murder. But, as I have said, that explanation did not satisfy me, and I still kept a lookout for new evidence connected with the waxworks.
===Chinese===
“这个阶段结束时，我们已有几分进展。我们已把一个无名之人同另一个无名之人认作了同一人，而后者毫无疑问是个职业罪犯。在那枚伪造的几尼里，我们为谋杀找到了一个说得通的动机。但正如我说过的，那个解释不能使我满意，我依旧留意着一切与蜡像相关的新证据。

===Original===
“The next stage opened on that night when you arrived at Cornishes’, looking like a resuscitated ‘found drowned.’ Your account of your fall into the canal and the immediately antecedent events made a deep impression on me, though I did not, at the time, connect them with the crime that we were investigating. But the whole affair was so abnormal that it seemed to call for very careful consideration; and the more I considered it the more abnormal did it appear.
===Chinese===
“下一个阶段，始于你到康尼什家的那个夜晚——你那副模样，活像一具还了魂的‘溺毙浮尸’。你对自己坠入运河的经过以及紧接在前的那几桩事的叙述，给了我极深的印象，尽管当时我并未把它们同我们正在查办的罪行联系起来。可是整件事太反常了，似乎非细加推敲不可；而我越推敲，它越显得反常。

===Original===
“The theory of an accident could not be entertained, nor could the dropping of that derrick have been a practical joke. Your objection that no one was in sight had no weight, since there was a gate in the wall by which a person could have made his escape. Someone had attempted to murder you: and that attempt had been made immediately after you had signed a cremation certificate. That was a very impressive fact. As you know, it is my habit to look very narrowly at cremation cases, for the reason that cremation offers great facilities for certain kinds of crime. Poisoners﻿—and particularly arsenic and antimony poisoners﻿—have repeatedly been convicted on evidence furnished by an exhumed body. If such poisoners can get the corpse of the victim cremated, they are virtually safe; for whatever suspicions may thereafter arise, no conviction is possible, since the means of proving the administration have been destroyed.
===Chinese===
“意外之说站不住，那根吊杆砸下来也决非恶作剧。你提出当时视野里空无一人，这条异议没有分量，因为墙上有一道门，人是可以从那里走脱的。有人蓄意谋害你：而这谋害，就发生在你签署一份火化证明书之后。这是一个非常触目的事实。你们知道，火化案我向来盯得极紧，因为火化为某几类罪行大开方便之门。投毒者——尤其是用砷、用锑的投毒者——不止一次是靠掘出的尸体提供的证据才定了罪。这类投毒者若能叫被害人的尸体烧成灰，便几乎安然无事了；因为此后无论起什么疑窦，定罪已无可能——证明投毒的物证已经毁掉了。

===Original===
“Accordingly, I considered very carefully your account of the proceedings, and as I did so strong suggestions of fraud arose in all directions. There was, for instance, the inspection window in the coffin. What was its object? Inspection windows are usually provided only in cases where the condition of the body is such that it has to be enclosed in a hermetically sealed coffin. But no such condition existed in this case. There was no reason why the friends should not have viewed the body in the usual manner in an open coffin. Again, there was the curious alternation of you and the two witnesses. First they went up and viewed deceased﻿—through the window. Then, after a considerable interval, you and Cropper went up and viewed deceased through the window. Then you took out the body, examined it, and put it back. Again, after a considerable interval, the witnesses went up a second time and viewed the deceased﻿—through the window.
===Chinese===
“因此，我把你叙述的那套程序仔细掂量了一番，这么掂量着，弄虚作假的疑云便从四面八方腾起来。比如，棺上那扇观察窗。它是干什么用的？观察窗通常只用于尸体状况非得密封入棺不可的场合。可本案并无这种状况。亲友们原可以照常开着棺盖瞻仰遗容，毫无理由不这么做。再者，你同两位见证人一递一换地进出，也颇为蹊跷。先是她们上楼，隔着观察窗看了死者。然后隔了老大一阵，你同克罗珀上楼，也隔着观察窗看死者。然后你把尸体抬出来，验看一番，再放回去。又隔了老大一阵，两位见证人再上楼，再看死者——还是隔着观察窗。

===Original===
“It was all rather queer and suspicious, especially when considered in conjunction with the attempt on your life. Reflecting on the latter, the question of the gate in the wall by the canal arose in my mind, and I examined the map to see if I could locate it. It was not marked, but the wharf was, and from this and your description it appeared certain that the gate must be in the wall of the garden of Morris’ house. Here was another suspicious fact. For Morris﻿—who could have let you out by this side gate﻿—sent you by a long, roundabout route to the towpath. He knew which way you must be going﻿—westward﻿—and could have slipped out of the gate and waited for you in the hut by the wharf. It was possible, and there seemed to be no other explanation of what had happened to you. Incidentally, I made another discovery. This map showed that Morris’ house had two frontages, one on Field Street and one on Market Street, and that you appeared to have been admitted by the back entrance. Which was another slightly abnormal circumstance.
===Chinese===
“这一切都相当古怪可疑，尤其同谋害你的企图对照着看。思索后者的时候，我心里冒出运河边墙上一道门的问题，便去查地图，想把它定位。图上没有标门，却标了那座码头；凭这一点，再加上你的描述，那道门必在莫里斯家花园的墙上，看来确凿无疑。这里又是一个可疑之处。因为莫里斯——他本可以放你从这道边门出去——却打发你绕了老大一个弯子才上纤道。他知道你必往哪边走——往西——他满可以从门里溜出去，在码头边的小屋里候着你。这是办得到的，而且你遭的殃似乎也没有别的解释。顺带说一句，我还有了个新发现。这张地图表明，莫里斯的房子有两个临街面，一面临菲尔德街，一面临市场街；而看来，你当初是由后门被让进去的。这又是一个略欠寻常的情形。

===Original===
“I was very much puzzled by the affair. There was a distinct suggestion that some fraud﻿—some deception﻿—had been practiced; that what the spinsters saw through the coffin window was not the same thing as that which you saw. And yet, what could the deception have been? There was no question about the body. It was a real body. The disease was undoubtedly genuine, and was, at least, the effective cause of death. And the cremation was necessarily genuine; for though you can bury an empty coffin, you can’t cremate one. The absence of calcined bone would expose the fraud instantly.
===Chinese===
“这件事教我大惑不解。种种情形分明暗示其中有诈——其中有瞒：两位独身女士隔着棺上观察窗看到的，同你看到的并不是同一件东西。然而，这瞒天过海能瞒的又是什么呢？尸体是无可置疑的。那是一具实实在在的尸体。疾病确凿无疑是真的，而且至少是死亡的直接原因。火化也必是真刀真枪的；因为空棺材你埋得，却火化不得。没有烧化的尸骨，骗局立刻便见分晓。

===Original===
“I considered the possibility of a second body; that of a murdered person, for instance. But that would not do. For if a substitution had been effected, there would still have been a redundant body to dispose of and account for. Nothing would have been gained by the substitution.
===Chinese===
“我考虑过第二具尸体的可能；譬如说，一个被害者的尸体。可这行不通。因为一旦掉了包，仍旧会多出一具需要销毁、需要交代的尸体。这一换，什么也落不着。

===Original===
“But there was another possibility to which no such objection applied. Assuming a fraud to have been perpetrated, here was a case adapted in the most perfect manner to the use of a waxwork. Of course, a full-length figure would have been impossible, because it would have left no calcined bones. But the inspection window would have made it unnecessary. A wax head would have done; or, better still, a wax mask, which could have been simply placed over the face of the real corpse. The more I thought about it the more was I impressed by the singular suitability of the arrangements to the use of a wax mask. The inspection window seemed to be designed for the very purpose﻿—to restrict the view to a mere face and to prevent the mask from being touched and the fraud thus discovered﻿—and the alternate inspections by you and the spinsters were quite in keeping with a deception of that kind.
===Chinese===
“但还有另一种可能，是不受这条异议掣肘的。假定确有欺诈发生，那么这个案子简直是给使用蜡像预备下的十全场合。当然，整身的蜡像不行，因为它留不下烧焦的尸骨。可观察窗恰恰让整身蜡像成了多余。一颗蜡制的头便够了；或者更妙些，一张蜡面模，往真尸的脸上一覆就是。我越想越觉得，那些安排同蜡面模的用途契合得蹊跷透顶。观察窗简直就是为此设计的——把视线限死在一张脸上，又叫面模摸不得，骗局由此无从败露——而你同两位独身女士轮番瞻仰，也同一类掉包把戏若合符节。

===Original===
“There was another very queer feature in the case. These people, living at Hoxton, elected to employ a doctor who lived miles away at Bloomsbury. Why did they not call in a neighbouring practitioner? Also, they arranged the days and even the hours at which the visits were to be made. Why? There was an evident suggestion of something that the doctor was not to know﻿—something or somebody that he was not desired to see; that some preparations had to be made for his visits.
===Chinese===
“本案还有一个极古怪的特点。这些人住在霍克斯顿，却偏要请一位住在几英里外布卢姆斯伯里（Bloomsbury）的医生。他们为何不请邻近的开业医生？而且，出诊的日子、连钟点都由他们排定。为什么？这里分明暗示着，有某样事情不能让医生知道——有某样东西或某个人，不愿让医生看见；暗示着每回出诊之前都得做些准备。

===Original===
“Again, the note was addressed to Dr. Stephen Gray, not to Dr. Cornish. They knew your name and address, although you had only just come there, and they did not know Dr. Cornish, who was an old resident. How was this? The only explanation seemed to be that they had read the report of the inquest, or even been present at it. You there stated publicly that your temporary address was at 61, Mecklenburgh Square; that you were, in fact, a bird of passage; and you gave your full name and your age. Now if any fraud was being carried out, a bird of passage, who might be difficult to find later, and a young one at that, was just the most suitable kind of doctor.
===Chinese===
“再者，那张便条上写的是斯蒂芬·格雷医生，不是康尼什医生。你才到那一带不久，他们就知道了你的姓名住址；而康尼什医生是老住户，他们反倒不认识。这是怎么说？唯一的解释似乎是：他们读过验尸审讯的报道，甚至到过堂。你在堂上公开说过，你的临时住址在梅克伦堡广场61号；也就是说，你是只过路候鸟；你还报了全名和年纪。要知道，倘若正有什么骗局在施行，一只过路候鸟——日后难寻踪迹，何况还是只年轻的候鸟——恰恰是最合式的医生。

===Original===
“To sum up the evidence at this stage: The circumstances, taken as a whole, suggested in the strongest possible manner that there was something fraudulent about this cremation. That fraud must be some kind of substitution or personation with the purpose of obtaining a certificate that some person had been cremated who had, in fact, not been cremated. In that case it was nearly certain that the dead man was not Simon Bendelow; for the certificates would be required to agree with the false appearances, not with the true. There was a suggestion﻿—but only a speculative one﻿—that the deception might have been effected by means of a wax mask.
===Chinese===
“把这一阶段的证据归结起来：种种情形合观，以最强不过的方式暗示这场火化有诈。那欺诈必是某种掉包或冒名顶替，目的是取得一份证明书，证明某个并未火化的人已经火化。果真如此，则棺中人几乎可以断定不是西蒙·本德洛；因为那些证明书须得同假象相符，而不是同真相相符。至于骗局的得逞或许凭借了一张蜡面模——这只是一个提示，不过是凭空的推测罢了。

===Original===
“There were, however, two objections. As to the wax mask, there was the great difficulty of obtaining one. A perfect portrait mask could have been obtained only either from an artist in Paris or from Julius D’Arblay. The objection to the substitution theory was that there was a real body﻿—the body of a real person. If the cremation was in a name which was not the name of that person, then the disappearance of that person would remain unaccounted for.
===Chinese===
“然而也有两条反对的理由。就蜡面模而论，难就难在极难得手。一张完美的肖像面模，除了从巴黎的艺术家那里，便只能从尤利乌斯·达布雷手里得到。就掉包说而论，反对的理由是那具尸体是真实的——一个真人的尸体。倘若火化用的是个名字，而那名字并非此人的名字，那么此人的失踪便永远无从交代了。

===Original===
“So you see that the whole theory of the fraud was purely conjectural. There was not a single particle of direct evidence. You also see that at two points there was a faint hint of a connection between this case and the murder of Mr. D’Arblay. These people seemed to have read of, or attended at the inquest; and if a wax mask existed, it was quite probably made by him.
===Chinese===
“所以你们看，这整套欺诈理论纯是出于推测。直接证据连一星半点也没有。你们也看到，在两个节点上，本案同达布雷先生遇害一案之间透着若有若无的牵连。这些人似乎读到过、或者到庭旁听过那次验尸审讯；而倘若真有一张蜡面模存在，它多半就是出自他的手。

===Original===
“The next stage opens with the discovery of the mask at the studio. But there are certain antecedent matters that must first be glanced at. When the attempt was made to murder Marion, I asked myself four questions: ‘1. Why did this man want to kill Marion? 2. What did he come to the studio on the preceding night to search for? 3. Did he find it, whatever it was? 4. Why did he delay so long to make the search?’
===Chinese===
“下一个阶段，以在画室里发现那副面模为开端。不过有几点前因，得先略作交代。谋害玛丽昂的企图发生时，我问过自己四个问题：‘1. 这人为什么想杀玛丽昂？2. 他前一夜到画室里来找的是什么？3. 不管那是什么，他找到没有？4. 他为什么拖延了这么久才来搜寻？’

===Original===
“Let us begin with the second question. What had he come to look for? The obvious suggestion was that he had come to get possession of some incriminating object. But what was that object? Could it be the mould of some forged coin or medal? I did not believe that it was. For since the forgery or forgeries were extant, the moulds had no particular significance; and what little significance they had applied to Mr. D’Arblay, who was, technically, the forger. My feeling was that the object was in some way connected with waxwork, and in all probability with a wax portrait mask, as the most likely thing to be used for a fraudulent purpose. And I need hardly say that the cremation case lurked in the back of my mind.
===Chinese===
“先说第二个问题。他是来找什么的？现成的答案是：他来是要把某件足以定罪的东西弄到手。可那件东西是什么？会不会是伪造钱币或章牌用的模子？我不信是这个。因为伪币既已流散在世，模子本身便没有多大干系；纵有那一点干系，牵扯的也是达布雷先生——从技术上讲，造假的是他。我的感觉是，那件东西以某种方式同蜡像相连，而且十有八九同一张蜡制肖像面模相连，因为那才是最可能派作诈骗用途的东西。至于火化一案一直蛰伏在我心底，这更不必说了。

===Original===
“This view was supported by consideration of the third question. Did he find what he came to seek? If he came for moulds of coins or medals, he must have found them; for none remained. But the fact that he came the next night and attempted to murder Marion﻿—believing her to be alone﻿—suggested that his search had failed. And consideration of the fourth question led﻿—less decisively﻿—to the same conclusion as to the nature of the object sought.
===Chinese===
“这个看法又得了对第三个问题的思考的助力。他找到他来寻的东西了吗？他若为钱币或章牌的模子而来，必定找到了；因为模子一副也没剩下。可是他第二天夜里又来，而且图谋杀害玛丽昂——他只当她独自在家——这一事实却说明他的搜寻落了空。而对第四个问题的思考——结论虽不那么斩钉截铁——也同样指向所寻之物的这般性质。

===Original===
“Why had he waited all this time to make the search? Why had he not entered the studio immediately after the murder, when the place was mostly unoccupied? The most probable explanation appeared to me to be that he had only recently become aware that there was any incriminating object in existence. Proceeding on the hypothesis that he had commissioned Mr. D’Arblay to make a wax portrait mask, I further assumed that he knew little of the process, and﻿—perhaps misunderstanding Mr. D’Arblay﻿—confused the technique of wax with that of plaster. In making a plaster mask from life﻿—as you probably know by this time﻿—you have to destroy the mould to get the mask out. So when the mask has been delivered to the client, there is nothing left.
===Chinese===
“他为什么拖延至今才来搜寻？谋杀之后那地方多半空着，他何不径直进画室去？依我看，最可能的解释是：直到不久之前，他才晓得世上存在着这么一件罪证。循着他曾委托达布雷先生制作一张蜡制肖像面模这一假设，我进一步假定：他对这道工序所知寥寥，而且——或许是错会了达布雷先生的意思——把蜡的工艺同石膏的工艺搅混了。从活人脸上翻制石膏面模——这层道理你们如今多半已经明白——必须把模子毁掉，才取得出面模。所以面模一旦交到主顾手里，便什么也不剩了。

===Original===
“But to make a wax mask, you must first make one of plaster to serve as a matrix from which to make the gelatine mould for the wax. Then, when the wax mask has been delivered to the client, the plaster matrix remains in the possession of the artist.
===Chinese===
“可要制作蜡面模，必得先制一张石膏的，充作母模，再用它翻制浇蜡用的明胶模。这样，蜡面模交到主顾手里之后，那副石膏母模仍留在艺术家手中。

===Original===
“The suggestion, then, was that this man had supposed that the mould had been destroyed in making the mask, and that only some time after the murder had he, in some way, discovered his mistake. When he did discover it, he would see what an appalling blunder he had made; for the plaster matrix was the likeness of his own face.
===Chinese===
“于是推测便是：此人原以为制面模时模子已经毁了，直到谋杀过后若干时日，才以某种方式发觉了自己的失算。他一发觉，便会看出自己闯下了何等可怕的大祸；因为那副石膏母模，正是他自己一张脸的留影。

===Original===
“You see that all this was highly speculative. It was all hypothetical, and it might all have been totally fallacious. We still had not a single solid fact; but all the hypothetical matter was consistent, and each inference seemed to support the others.”
===Chinese===
“你们看，这一切全出于推测。清一色是假设，也可能满盘皆错。我们手里仍旧没有一件过硬的事实；但全部假设材料彼此吻合，每一条推论似乎都在给其余的推论作保。”

===Original===
“And what,” I asked, “did you suppose was his motive for trying to make away with Marion?”
===Chinese===
“那么，”我问，“您当时揣想，他要除掉玛丽昂，动机又是什么呢？”

===Original===
“In the first place,” he replied, “I inferred that he looked on her as a dangerous person who might have some knowledge of his transactions with her father. This was probably the explanation of his attempt when he cut the brake-wire of her bicycle. But the second, more desperate attack, was made, I assume, when he had realized the existence of the plaster mask, and supposed that she knew of it, too. If he had killed her, he would probably have made another search with the studio fully lighted up.
===Chinese===
“首先，”他答道，“我推断他把玛丽昂看作一个危险人物，怕她对她父亲同他之间的交易有所知晓。他剪断她自行车闸线的那次图谋，多半便是这个缘故。而第二次更下狠手的袭击，我推想是在他获知石膏面模的存在、又疑心她也知情之后下的手。他若杀了她，多半还要打着灯、把画室照个通明，再搜上一遍。”

===Original===
“To return to our inquiry. You see that I had a mass of hypothesis but not a single real fact. But I still had a firm belief that a wax mask had been made and that﻿—if it had not been destroyed﻿—there must be a plaster mask somewhere in the studio. That was what I came to look for that morning; and as it happens that I am some six inches taller than Bendelow was, I was able to see what had been invisible to him. When I discovered that mask, and when Marion had disclaimed all knowledge of it, my hopes began to rise. But when you identified the face as that of Morris, I felt that our problem was solved. In an instant, my card-house of speculative hypothesis was changed into a solid edifice. What had been but bare possibilities had now become so highly probable that they were almost certainties.
===Chinese===
“回到我们的正题。你们看，我怀里抱着一大堆假设，却没有一件真正的事实。可我仍旧坚信：一张蜡面模是做下了的；而且——只要它没有销毁——画室里某处必定有一副石膏面模。那天早晨我来寻的正是它；又碰巧我比本德洛高出六英寸上下，便能看到他看不到的地方。待我发现那副面模，而玛丽昂又声明对它一无所知，我的希望便开始升腾。而当你认出那张脸正是莫里斯，我便知道我们的难题解开了。霎时间，我那座纸牌搭的假设之屋变成了一座结结实实的大厦。先前不过是些光秃秃的可能，如今已大概率得近乎确凿了。

===Original===
“Let us consider what the finding of this mask proved﻿—subject, of course, to verification. It proved that a wax mask of Morris had been made﻿—for here was the matrix, varnished, as you will remember, in readiness for the gelatine mould; and that mask was obviously obtained for the purpose of a fraudulent cremation. And that mask was made by Julius D’Arblay.
===Chinese===
“我们且想想，这副面模的发现证明了什么——当然，还有待验证。它证明了一张莫里斯的蜡面模被制作过——因为母模就在这里，你们记得，还上过漆，备着翻明胶模用；而那张面模，显是为了行一场有诈的火化才弄到手的。而制作那张面模的，正是尤利乌斯·达布雷。

===Original===
“What was the purpose of the fraud? It was perfectly obvious. Morris was clearly the real Simon Bendelow, and the purpose of the fraud was to create undeniable evidence that he was dead. But why did he want to prove that he was dead? Well, we knew that he was the murderer of Van Zellen, for whom the American police were searching, and he might be in more danger than we knew. At any rate, a death certificate would make him absolutely secure﻿—on one condition﻿—that the body was cremated. Mere burial would not be enough; for an exhumation would discover the fraud. But perfect security could be secured only by destruction of all evidence of the fraud. Julius D’Arblay held such evidence. Therefore Julius D’Arblay must be got rid of. Here, then, was an amply sufficient motive for the murder. The only point which remained obscure was the identity of your patient, and the means by which his disappearance had been accounted for.
===Chinese===
“那骗局图的是什么？明摆着。莫里斯显然就是真正的西蒙·本德洛，骗局的目的，便是造出无可辩驳的证据，证明他已经死了。可他为什么要证明自己死了呢？喏，我们知道他是杀害范泽伦的凶手，美国警方正在缉拿他，而且他身陷的危险，也许比我们知道的还多。无论如何，一张死亡证明书能保他万全——只消一个条件——尸体烧掉。单是埋了不够；因为掘尸一验，骗局立现。而万全，唯有把骗局的一切证据统统毁灭才保得住。尤利乌斯·达布雷手里就捏着这样的证据。所以尤利乌斯·达布雷必须除掉。于是，谋杀便有了再充分不过的动机。仅剩的一点不明之处是：你的病人究竟是谁，以及他的失踪是用了什么法子交代的。

===Original===
“My hypothesis, then, had been changed into highly probable theory. The next stage was the necessary verification. I began with a rather curious experiment. The man who tried to murder Marion could have been no other than her father’s murderer. Then he must have been Morris. But it seemed that he was totally unlike Morris, and the mask evidently suggested to her no resemblance. But yet it was probable that the man was Morris, for the striking features﻿—the hook nose and the heavy brows﻿—would be easily ‘made up,’ especially at night. The question was whether the face was Morris’s with these additions. I determined to put that question to the test. And here Polton’s new accomplishment came to our aid.
===Chinese===
“这样，我的假设便升格为高度可靠的理论。下一阶段是必不可少的验证。我从一个颇为奇特的实验着手。企图谋杀玛丽昂的那个人，只可能是她父亲的凶手。那么他就必定是莫里斯。可是那人看去同莫里斯毫无相似之处，那副面模显然也没让她起过半点相像的念头。然而那人多半就是莫里斯，因为那几处惹眼的特征——鹰钩鼻和浓眉——是极容易‘化装’出来的，尤其在天黑之后。问题在于：那张脸是不是添了这几笔的莫里斯的脸。我决意拿这个问题一试。这时，波尔顿的新手艺来搭手了。

===Original===
“First, with a pinch of clay, we built up on Morris’s mask a nose of the shape described and slightly thickened the brows. Then Polton made a gelatine mould, and from this produced a wax mask. He fitted it with glass eyes and attached it to a rough plaster head, with ears which were casts of my own painted. We then fixed on a moustache, beard, and wig, and put on a shirt, collar, and jacket. It was an extraordinarily crude affair, suggestive of the fifth of November. But it answered the purpose, which was to produce a photograph; for we made the photograph so bad﻿—so confused and ill-focused﻿—that the crudities disappeared, while the essential likeness remained. As you know, that photograph was instantly recognized, without any sort of suggestion. So the first test gave a positive result. Marion’s assailant was pretty certainly Morris.”
===Chinese===
“我们先取一小撮黏土，在莫里斯的面模上堆出一个所说的鼻子形状，又把眉毛略略加浓。随后波尔顿翻了一副明胶模，用它制出一张蜡面模。他给它配上玻璃假眼，装到一颗粗制的石膏头上——那头的耳朵是照我本人的耳朵翻的模，涂了色。然后我们安上髭须、胡髯和假发，给它套上衬衫、领子和外衣。那是一件粗糙得出奇的东西，颇有几分十一月五日篝火夜假人的味道。可它顶了用，目的原在拍一张照片；因为我们把照片拍得那样糟——那样混沌、那样失焦——粗陋处尽皆隐去，而根本的相像却留了下来。你们知道，那张照片人家一眼便认了出来，不曾受半点提示。于是第一项检验得了个正果。袭击玛丽昂的人，几乎可以断定就是莫里斯。”

===Original===
“I should like to have seen Mr. Polton’s ’prentice effort,” said Marion, who had been listening, enthralled by this description.
===Chinese===
“我真想看看波尔顿先生那件‘学徒习作’，”玛丽昂说；她方才听着这段描写，听得入了迷。

===Original===
“You shall see it now,” Thorndyke replied with a smile. “It is in the next room, concealed in a cupboard.”
===Chinese===
“你现在就可以看，”桑代克含笑答道。“它就在隔壁屋里，藏在一只柜橱中。”

===Original===
He went out, and presently returned, carrying what looked like an excessively crude hairdresser’s dummy, but a most extraordinarily horrible and repulsive one. As he turned the face towards us, Marion gave a little cry of horror and then tried to laugh﻿—without very striking success.
===Chinese===
他走了出去，须臾捧着一件东西回来，看去像个粗劣之极的理发店假人头，却又是骇人至极、可憎至极的一件。他把那张脸转向我们时，玛丽昂发出一声小小的惊呼，随即想笑一笑——却笑得颇不见效。

===Original===
“It is a dreadful-looking thing!” she exclaimed; “and so hideously like that fiend.” She gazed at it with the most extreme repugnance for a while, and then said, apologetically: “I hope you won’t think me very silly, but﻿—”
===Chinese===
“这东西样子真吓人！”她叫道；“而且像那个恶魔，像得吓人。”她怀着极度的憎恶对它瞪视了半晌，然后抱歉似的说：“希望您别嫌我太傻气，可是——”

===Original===
“Of course I don’t,” Thorndyke interrupted. “It is going back to its cupboard at once;” and with this he bore it away, returning in a few moments with a smaller object, wrapped in a cloth, which he laid on the table. “Another ‘exhibit,’ as they say in the courts,” he explained, “which we shall want presently. Meanwhile we resume the thread of our argument.
===Chinese===
“当然不会，”桑代克打断她。“它这就回柜橱里去；”说着他把它捧走了，须臾又捧着一件小些的东西回来，裹在一块布里，放在桌上。“另一件‘证物’，法庭上是这么叫的，”他解释道，“待一会儿我们用得着。眼下且把我们论证的线头再接上。

===Original===
“The photograph of this waxwork, then, furnished corroboration of the theory that Morris was the man whom we were seeking. My next move was to inquire at Scotland Yard if there were any fresh developments of the Van Zellen case. The answer was that there were; and Superintendent Miller arranged to come and tell me all about them. You were present at the interview and will remember what passed. His information was highly important, not only by confirming my inference that Bendelow was the murderer, but especially by disposing of the difficulty connected with the disappearance of your patient. For now there came into view a second man﻿—Crile﻿—who had died at Hoxton of an abdominal cancer and had been duly buried; and when you were able to give me this man’s address, a glance at the map and at the Post Office Directory showed that the two men had died in the same house. This fact, with the further facts that they had died of virtually the same disease and within a day or two of the same date, left no reasonable doubt that we were really dealing with one man, who had died and for whom two death certificates, in different names, and two corresponding burial orders, had been obtained. There was only one body, and that was cremated in the name of Bendelow. It followed that the coffin which was buried at Mr. Crile’s funeral must have been an empty coffin. I was so confident that this must be so that I induced Miller to apply for an exhumation, with the results that you know.
===Chinese===
“这张蜡像的照片，为莫里斯正是我们追寻之人的理论添了佐证。我的下一步，是到苏格兰场打听范泽伦案有无新的进展。答复是有；米勒警司便安排前来，把详情一一相告。那次晤谈你在座，谈了些什么你记得。他带来的消息至关重要，不但坐实了本德洛是凶手这个推断，尤其排解了与你那位病人之失踪相关的疑难。因为这时又浮出一个第二者——克赖尔——他在霍克斯顿死于腹部癌症，已如期下葬；而当你能把此人的住址告诉我时，对地图和邮政人名地址录（Post Office Directory）略一查考便见分晓：两个男人死在同一座房子里。这一事实，再加上他们死于几乎相同的疾病、日期只差一两天，便使事情无可置疑：我们真正对付的是同一个人——他死了，而有人用两个不同的名字为他领到了两张死亡证明书和相应的两份下葬许可。尸体只有一具，是用本德洛的名字火化的。由此可知，克赖尔先生葬礼上埋下去的那口棺材，必是一口空棺。我对此确信不疑，于是撺掇米勒申请掘尸，其结果你们都已经知道了。

===Original===
“There now remained only a single point requiring verification: the question as to what face it was that those two ladies saw when they looked into the coffin of Simon Bendelow. Here again Polton’s new accomplishments came to our aid. From the plaster mask your apprentice made a most realistic wax mask, which I offer for your critical inspection.”
===Chinese===
“这时只剩下一个有待验证的疑点：那两位女士往西蒙·本德洛的棺材里看的时候，看到的究竟是怎样一张脸。这里又是波尔顿的新手艺来帮忙。你的学徒用那副石膏面模翻出了一张逼真至极的蜡面模，我把它呈上，请二位细细品鉴。”

===Original===
He unfolded the cloth and produced a mask of thin, yellowish wax and of a most cadaverous aspect, which he handed to Marion.
===Chinese===
他展开布，取出一副面模——蜡质轻薄，色泽微黄，一副死人相——递到玛丽昂手里。

===Original===
“Yes,” she said approvingly, “it is an excellent piece of work; and what beautiful eyelashes. They look exactly like real ones.”
===Chinese===
“是啊，”她赞许地说，“活计做得真精；这睫毛多漂亮啊，瞧上去同真的一模一样。”

===Original===
“They are real ones,” Thorndyke explained with a chuckle.
===Chinese===
“本来就是真的，”桑代克轻笑着道明。

===Original===
She looked up at him inquiringly, and then, breaking into a ripple of laughter, exclaimed: “Of course! They are his own! Oh! How like Mr. Polton. But he was quite right, you know. He couldn’t have got the effect any other way.”
===Chinese===
她询问似的抬眼望了望他，随即漾起一阵清脆的笑声，喊道：“当然是啦！是他自己的睫毛！哎呀！多像波尔顿先生行事。不过他做得一点不错，您知道。不用这个法子，那效果他做不出来。”

===Original===
“So he declared,” said Thorndyke. “Well, we hired a coffin and had an inspection window put in the lid, and we got a black skull cap. We put a dummy head in the coffin with a wig on it; we laid the mask where the face should have been, and we adjusted the jaw-bandage and the skull cap so as to cover up the edges of the mask, and we got the two ladies here and showed them the coffin. When they had identified the tenant as Mr. Bendelow, the verification was complete. The hypothesis was now converted into ascertained fact, and all that remained to be done was to lay hands on the murderer.”
===Chinese===
“他正是这么说的，”桑代克道。“于是我们赁了一口棺材，在棺盖上开了观察窗，又置办了一顶黑丝小帽。我们把一颗戴着假发的假头放进棺材；把面模安在脸应在之处；再把下颌绷带和黑丝小帽调摆停当，把面模的边缘都掩住；然后把两位女士请到此处，让她们看那口棺材。待她们认明棺中人是本德洛先生，验证便告完成。至此，假说一变而为查明的事实；剩下的，只消伸手捉拿凶手了。”

===Original===
“How did you find out where Morris was living?” I asked.
===Chinese===
“你们是怎么探得莫里斯住处的？”我问。

===Original===
“Barber did that,” he replied. “When I learned that you were being stalked, I employed Barber to shadow you. He, of course, observed Morris on your track and followed him home.”
===Chinese===
“那是巴伯办的，”他答道。“我一听说有人盯你的梢，便雇了巴伯来盯你。他自然看见了尾随你的莫里斯，便一路跟他到了家。”

===Original===
“That was what I supposed,” said I; and for a while we were all silent. Presently Marion said: “It is all very involved and confusing. Would you mind telling us exactly what happened?”
===Chinese===
“我猜也是这样，”我说；有一阵子我们都默然。过了片刻，玛丽昂说：“这些事千头万绪，把人都绕糊涂了。可否请您把事情的原委原原本本讲给我们听？”

===Original===
“In a direct narrative, you mean?” said he. “Yes, I will try to reconstruct the events in the order of their occurrence. They began with the murder of Van Zellen by Bendelow. There was no evidence against him at the time, but he had to fly from America for other reasons, and he left behind him incriminating traces which he knew must presently be discovered, and which would fix the murder on him. His friend, Crile, who fled with him, developed gastric cancer, and had only a month or two to live. Then Bendelow decided that when Crile should die, he would make believe to die at the same time. To this end, he commissioned your father to make a wax mask﻿—a portrait mask of himself with the eyes closed. His wife must then have persuaded the two spinsters to visit him﻿—he, of course, taking to his bed when they called, and being represented as a mortally sick man. Then he moved from Hornsey to Hoxton, taking Crile with him. There he engaged two doctors﻿—Usher and Gray, both of whom lived at a distance﻿—to attend Crile, and to visit him on alternate days. Crile seems to have been deaf, or, at least, hard of hearing, and was kept continuously under the influence of morphia. Usher, who was employed by Mrs. Bendelow, whom he knew as Mrs. Pepper, came to the front of the house, in Field Street, to visit Mr. Crile, while Stephen﻿—who was employed by the Bendelows, whom he knew by the name of Morris﻿—entered at the rear of the house in Market Street, to visit the same man under the name of Bendelow. About the time of the move Bendelow committed the murder in order to destroy all evidence of the making of the wax mask.
===Chinese===
“你是说要一份直陈始末的叙述？”他说。“好，我试着把种种事件按发生的先后次第复原出来。事情发端于本德洛谋杀范泽伦。当时虽无对他不利的证据，他却因别的缘故不得不逃出美国，而且留下了罪迹，他知道它们不久必然被发现，会把那桩凶案坐实在他头上。同他一道出逃的朋友克赖尔得了胃癌，只剩一两个月的寿数。于是本德洛打定主意：克赖尔死时，他便装作与之同时殒命。为达此目的，他委托你父亲制作一张蜡面模——一张他本人阖着眼的肖像面模。随后必是他妻子劝动了那两位独身女士前来探望——她们登门时，他自然已卧床不起，被说成是个病入膏肓的人。随后他携着克赖尔，从霍恩西迁往霍克斯顿。到那里，他延请了两位医生——厄舍和格雷，两人都住得远——来给克赖尔治病，隔日轮流出诊。克赖尔似乎耳聋，至少重听，而且一直沉在吗啡的药力之下。厄舍受雇于本德洛太太——他认得她叫佩珀太太——从临菲尔德街的前门进屋，探视克赖尔先生；斯蒂芬——他受雇于本德洛夫妇，却认得他们姓莫里斯——则从临市场街的后门进来，以本德洛的名义探视同一个人。大约在迁居前后，本德洛下了毒手，为的是销毁制作蜡面模的一切痕迹。

===Original===
“Eventually Crile died﻿—or was finished off with an extra dose of morphia﻿—on a Thursday. Usher gave the certificate, and the funeral took place on the Saturday. But previously﻿—probably on the Friday night﻿—the coffin-lid was unscrewed by Bendelow, the body taken out and replaced by a sack of sawdust with some lead pipe in it.
===Chinese===
“克赖尔终于死了——或者被额外一剂吗啡送了终——死在一个星期四。厄舍开了证明书，葬礼在星期六举行。但在这之前——多半在星期五夜里——本德洛旋开了棺盖，把尸体取出，换上一袋掺了几截铅管的锯末。

===Original===
“On the Monday the body was again produced: this time as that of Simon Bendelow, who was represented as having died on the Sunday afternoon. It was put in a cremation coffin with a celluloid window in the lid. The wax mask was placed over the face; the jaw-bandage and the skull cap adjusted to hide the place where the wax face joined the real face; and the two spinsters were brought up to see Mr. Bendelow in his coffin. They looked in through the window, and, of course, saw the wax mask of Bendelow. They then retired. The coffin-lid was taken off, the wax mask removed, the coffin-lid screwed on again, and then the two doctors were brought up. They removed the body from the coffin, examined it, and put it back; and Bendelow﻿—or Morris﻿—put on the coffin-lid.
===Chinese===
“到了星期一，尸体再度登场：这回充作西蒙·本德洛的遗体，被说成是星期日下午死的。它被装进一口火化棺，棺盖上嵌着赛璐珞观察窗。蜡面模覆在脸上；下颌绷带同黑丝小帽调摆停当，掩住蜡脸同真脸相接之处；然后两位独身女士被请上楼，来看棺中的本德洛先生。她们隔着窗往里瞧，看见的当然是本德洛的蜡面模。随后她们退下。棺盖卸下，蜡面模取走，棺盖重新旋上，然后两位医生被请上楼。他们把遗体从棺中抬出，验看一番，再放回去；由本德洛——或者说莫里斯——把棺盖安上。

===Original===
“As soon as the doctors were gone, the coffin-lid was taken off again, the wax mask was put back and adjusted, and the coffin-lid replaced and screwed down finally. Then the two ladies were brought up again to take a last look at poor Mr. Bendelow; not actually the last look, for, at the funeral they peeped in at the window and saw the wax face just before the coffin was passed through into the crematorium.”
===Chinese===
“医生们一走，棺盖再度卸下，蜡面模重新覆上、摆正，棺盖又盖回去，这一回旋死了。然后两位女士再被请上楼，同可怜的本德洛先生作最后一面；其实还算不得最后一面，因为出殡时她们还隔着观察窗窥了一眼，在棺材被抬进火化间的门之前，又看了那张蜡脸一眼。”

===Original===
“It was a diabolically clever scheme,” said I.
===Chinese===
“这真是个魔鬼也似奸狡的计划，”我说。

===Original===
“It was,” he agreed. “It was perfectly convincing and consistent. If you and those two ladies had been put in the witness-box, your testimony and theirs would have been in complete agreement. They had seen Simon Bendelow (whom they knew quite well) in his coffin. A few minutes later, you had seen Simon Bendelow in his coffin, had taken the body out, examined it thoroughly, and put it back, and had seen the coffin-lid screwed down, and again a few minutes later, they had looked in through the coffin-window and had again seen Simon Bendelow. The evidence would appear to be beyond the possibility of a doubt. Simon Bendelow was proved conclusively to be dead and cremated and was doubly certified to have died from natural causes. Nothing could be more complete.
===Chinese===
“可不是，”他深以为然。“它天衣无缝、首尾一贯。倘若你和那两位女士被传上证人席，你们的证词会字字吻合。她们见过西蒙·本德洛（她们同他相熟得很）躺在棺中。几分钟后，你也见过西蒙·本德洛躺在棺中，把遗体抬出来通身验看、又放了回去，还眼看着棺盖旋死；再过几分钟，她们又隔着棺上观察窗张了一眼，又见到了西蒙·本德洛。这证据看来不容一丝置疑。西蒙·本德洛被确凿证明已死、已焚，还有两纸证明书作双保险，证明他死于自然原因。再完备也没有了。

===Original===
“And yet,” he continued, after a pause, “while we are impressed by the astonishing subtlety and ingenuity displayed, we are almost more impressed by the fundamental stupidity exhibited along with it; a stupidity that seems to be characteristic of this type of criminal. For all the security that was gained by one part of the scheme was destroyed by the idiotic efforts to guard against dangers that had no existence. The murder was not only a foul crime; it was a technical blunder of the most elementary kind. But for that murder, Bendelow would now be alive and in unchallenged security. The cremation scheme was completely successful. It deceived everybody. Even the two detectives, though they felt vague suspicions, saw no loophole. They had to accept the appearances at their face value.
===Chinese===
“然而，”他顿了顿，接下去说，“我们在叹服这计划所显示的惊人阴巧与匠心之余，几乎更叹服与之并存的那份根本性的愚蠢；这份愚蠢，似乎正是这一类罪犯的标记。因为计划的一部分挣来的万全，全教那些防备子虚乌有之危险的蠢功夫给断送了。那桩谋杀不仅是一桩肮脏的罪；它还是最低级、最起码意义上的技术失误。若不是那桩谋杀，本德洛此刻还活得好好的，稳坐在无人置疑的安然里。火化计划是完全得手的。它骗过了所有人。连那两名警探，尽管隐约生疑，也瞧不出破绽。他们只得按表象的面值照单全收。

===Original===
“But it was the old story. The wrongdoer could not keep quiet. He must be forever making himself safer and yet more safe. And at each move, he laid down fresh tracks. And so, in the end, he delivered himself into our hands.”
===Chinese===
“可这又是老一套的故事了。作恶的人不肯安分。他定要把自己弄得安全了还要更安全。而每挪一步，他都布下新的踪迹。于是到头来，他亲手把自己送进了我们的掌握。”

===Original===
He paused and for a while seemed to be absorbed in reflection on what he had been telling us. Presently he looked up, and addressing Marion, said in grave, quiet tones:
===Chinese===
他停了下来，有一阵子似乎只顾默想方才对我们讲过的一切。不久他抬起头来，对着玛丽昂，用庄重而安静的声调说道：

===Original===
“We have ended our quest and we have secured retribution. Justice was beyond our reach, for complete justice implies restitution; and to attain that, the dead must have been recalled from the grave. But, at least sometimes, out of evil cometh good. Surely it will seem to you, when, in the happy years which I trust and confidently believe lie before you, your thoughts turn back to the days of your mourning and grief, that the beloved father, who, when living, made your happiness his chief concern, even in dying, bequeathed to you a blessing.”
===Chinese===
“我们的追寻已经到头，我们也已经讨得报应。完全的正义非我们力所能及，因为完全的正义意味着复原；而要复原，逝者就必须从坟墓里唤回。但邪恶之中，至少有时也能生出善来。当你在幸福的岁月里——我深信、也笃定那岁月就在你前面——回首这段服丧与哀恸的日子时，你必定会觉得：那位生前把你的幸福当作头等大事的慈父，即使到了临终，也遗赠给你一份祝福。”

## XV Thorndyke Proposes a New Move / 第十五章 桑代克提出一步新棋

===Original===
On the following morning, in order to make sure of arriving before the detective officer, I presented myself at King’s Bench Walk a good half-hour before I was due. The door was opened by Thorndyke himself, and as we shook hands he said: “I am glad you have come early, Gray. No doubt Polton explained the programme to you, but I should like to make our position quite clear. The officer who is coming here presently is Detective-Superintendent Miller, of the Criminal Investigation Department. He is quite an old friend, and he is coming at my request to give me certain information. But, of course, he is a detective officer, with his own duties to his department, and an exceedingly shrewd, capable man. Naturally, if he can pick up any crumbs of information from us, he will; and I don’t want him to learn more, at present, than I choose to tell him.”
===Chinese===
第二天早晨，为了稳稳赶在那位侦探警官之前到达，我比约定时间提早了足足半个钟头，来到王座法庭步道。开门的正是桑代克本人，握手时他说道：“你来得早，我很高兴，格雷。波尔顿无疑已经把今天的日程向你交代过了，不过我还是想把我们的处境说得清清楚楚。即刻要来此地的这位警官，是刑事调查部的侦缉警司（Detective-Superintendent）米勒。他是一位老朋友了，这回是应我之邀，来向我提供某些情况的。不过，他当然是个侦探警官，对自己部门负有职责，而且是位极其精明干练的人物。倘若能从我们这里捡到一星半点的消息，他自然不会放过；眼下，我不愿让他知道的，超出我愿意告诉他的范围。”

===Original===
“Why do you want to keep him in the dark?” I asked.
===Chinese===
“你为什么想瞒着他？”我问。

===Original===
“Because,” he replied, “we are doing quite well, and I want to get the case complete before I call in the police. If I were to tell him all I know and all I think, he might get too busy, and scare our man away before we have enough evidence to justify an arrest. As soon as the investigation is finished, and we have such evidence as will secure a conviction, I shall turn the case over to him; meanwhile, we keep our own counsel. Your role this morning will be that of listener. Whatever happens, make no comment. Act as if you knew nothing that is not of public knowledge.”
===Chinese===
“因为，”他答道，“我们眼下办得很顺，我想把案子做到完备，再去请警方介入。倘若我把知道的一切、猜想的一切都告诉他，他也许会忙过了头，在我们还没拿到足以证明逮捕正当的证据之前，先把那人吓跑。等调查完结、我们掌握了足以定罪的证据，我会把案子移交给他；在此之前，我们守口如瓶。今天上午，你的角色是听客。无论发生什么，一概不要置评。就当自己除了人尽皆知的事以外，一无所知。”

===Original===
I promised to follow his directions to the letter, though I could not get rid of the feeling that all this secrecy was somewhat futile. Then I began to tell him of my experiences of the previous night, to which he listened at first with grave interest, but with growing amusement as the story developed. When I came to the final chase and the pursuing policeman, he leaned back in his chair and laughed heartily.
===Chinese===
我答应一切照他的嘱咐办，只是心里总拂不去一种感觉：这般守密未免近乎无谓。接着我开始向他讲头天夜里的经历，他起初听得郑重而专注，随着故事铺展开来，神色里的笑意却越来越浓。讲到最后的追逐和那位紧追不舍的警察时，他往椅背上一靠，敞怀大笑起来。

===Original===
“Why,” he exclaimed, wiping his eyes, “it was a regular procession! It only wanted a string of sausages and a harlequin to bring it up to pantomime form.”
===Chinese===
“哎呀，”他抹着眼睛叫道，“那分明是一支像模像样的游行队伍！只差一串香肠、一个丑角，就能凑成一场地道的哑剧了。”

===Original===
“Yes,” I admitted with a grin, “it was a ludicrous affair. But it was a mighty mysterious affair too. You see, neither of the men was the man I had expected. There must be more people in this business than we had supposed. Have you any idea who these men can be?”
===Chinese===
“是啊，”我咧嘴承认，“事情是够荒唐的。可也神秘得可以。你要知道，那两个人里，没有一个是我原先料想的那位。这桩事里涉足的人，必定比我们设想的多。你想得出这两个人会是谁吗？”

===Original===
“It isn’t much use making vague guesses,” he replied. “The important point to note is that this incident, farcical as it turned out, might easily have taken a tragical turn; and the moral is that for the present you can’t be too careful in keeping out of harm’s way.”
===Chinese===
“漫无边际地瞎猜没有多大用处，”他答道，“要紧的是记住：这场风波虽说收场收得滑稽，却极可能轻易转成悲剧；教训便是，眼下你避灾远祸，怎样小心都不为过。”

===Original===
It was obvious to me that he was evading my question; that those two sinister strangers were not the mystery to him that they were to me, and I was about to return to the charge with a more definitely pointed question when an elaborate flourish on the little brass knocker of the inner door announced a visitor.
===Chinese===
我看得明白，他这是在回避我的问题；那两个面目阴沉的陌生人，于他并不像于我那样是桩谜。我正要卷土重来，把问题问得更加直截了当，内门的小铜环上忽然响起一串繁复花哨的敲击，通报有客来到。

===Original===
The tall, military-looking man whom Thorndyke admitted was evidently the Superintendent, as I gathered from the mutual greetings. He looked rather hard at me until Thorndyke introduced me, which he did with characteristic reticence.
===Chinese===
桑代克迎进来的那个人，个子高高的，一副军人气概，从两人寒暄的样子看，显然便是那位警司。他相当严厉地打量着我，直到桑代克将我引见——引见之词带着他惯有的简约。

===Original===
“This is Dr. Gray, Miller; you may remember his name. It was he who discovered the body of Mr. D’Arblay.”
===Chinese===
“这位是格雷博士，米勒；你或许还记得这个名字。发现达布雷先生尸体的，正是他。”

===Original===
“Yes, I remember,” said the Superintendent, shaking my hand unemotionally and still looking at me with a slightly dubious air.
===Chinese===
“是的，我记得，”警司说着，不动声色地同我握了握手，望着我时神色里仍带着一点将信将疑。

===Original===
“He is a good deal interested in the case,” Thorndyke continued, “not only professionally, but as a friend of the family﻿—since the catastrophe.”
===Chinese===
“他对这案子颇为关心，”桑代克接着说，“不但是职业上的关心，还因为他是这家人的朋友——自那场大祸以来。”

===Original===
“I see,” said the Superintendent, taking a final inquisitive look at me and obviously wondering why the deuce I was there. “Well, there is nothing of a very secret nature in what I have to tell you, and I suppose you can rely on Dr. Gray to keep his own counsel and ours.”
===Chinese===
“原来如此，”警司说着，朝我投来最后一道探询的目光，分明在纳闷我究竟上这儿来做什么。“不过，我要讲的事情里没有什么高度机密的货色，想必格雷博士也守得住口风——守他自己的，也守我们的。”

===Original===
“Certainly,” replied Thorndyke. “He quite understands that our talk is confidential, even if it is not secret.”
===Chinese===
“那自然，”桑代克答道，“他很明白，我们的谈话是机密的——即便算不上秘密。”

===Original===
The officer nodded, and, having been inducted into an easy chair, by the side of which a decanter, a siphon, and a box of cigars had been placed, settled himself comfortably, lit a cigar, mixed himself a modest refresher, and drew from his pocket a bundle of papers secured with red tape.
===Chinese===
警官点了点头，被让进一张安乐椅——椅边早已摆好一只酒樽、一瓶苏打水和一匣雪茄——他舒舒服服坐定，点上一支雪茄，给自己调了浅浅一杯醒神的酒，然后从口袋里掏出一束用红带子扎着的文件。

===Original===
“You asked me, Doctor,” he began, “to get you all particulars up to date of the Van Zellen case. Well, I can do that without difficulty as the case﻿—or at least what is left of it﻿—is in my hands. The circumstances of the actual crime I think you know already, so I will take up the story from that point.
===Chinese===
“博士，”他开了腔，“您托我把范泽伦案迄今的一切详情都弄到手。这个不难，因为这件案子——或者说至少是它剩下的部分——就攥在我手里。案情本身的原委，想必您已经知道了，我便从那一点接着往下讲。

===Original===
“Van Zellen, as you know, was found dead in his room, poisoned with prussic acid, and a quantity of very valuable portable property was missing. It was not clear whether the murderer had let himself in with false keys or whether Van Zellen had let him in; but the place hadn’t been broken into. The job had been done with remarkable skill, so that not a trace of the murderer was left. Consequently, all that was left for the police to do was to consider whether they knew of anyone whose methods agreed with those of this murderer.
===Chinese===
“如您所知，范泽伦被发现死在自己房里，中的是氢氰酸的毒，一批十分贵重的轻便财物失了踪。凶手究竟是持假钥匙自己开门进来的，还是范泽伦亲自放他进来的，无从判明；但那宅子并未遭撬闯。活儿干得异常利落，凶手未留半点痕迹。于是，警方所能做的只剩一件事：想想他们可知道有什么人，其手法与这凶手的手法对得上号。

===Original===
“Well, they did know of such a person, but they had nothing against him but suspicion. He had never been convicted of any serious crime, though he had been in chokee once or twice for receiving. But there had been a number of cases of robbery with murder﻿—or rather murder with robbery, for this man seemed to have committed the murder as a preliminary precaution﻿—and they were all of this kind; a solitary crime, very skilfully carried out by means of poison. There was never any trace of the criminal; but gradually the suspicions of the police settled down on a rather mysterious individual of the name of Bendelow; Simon Bendelow. Consequently, when the Van Zellen crime came to light, they were inclined to put it on this man Bendelow, and they began making fresh inquiries about him. But presently it transpired that someone had seen a man, on the morning of the crime, coming away from the neighbourhood of Van Zellen’s house just about the time when the murder must have been committed.”
===Chinese===
“这样的人，他们倒真知道一个，可是除嫌疑之外，拿不出别的东西来对付他。他从没因重罪被判过刑，只是为销赃蹲过一两次局子。但那一向有几桩劫杀案——或者不如说杀劫案，因为此人似乎把杀人当作先发制人的防备——而且手法全是一个路数：独行作案，借毒药之手，干得干净利落。罪犯从不留踪迹；但警方的疑心渐渐落在一个颇为神秘的人物身上，此人姓本德洛——西蒙·本德洛。于是，范泽伦凶案一披露，他们便倾向于把这案子记到这个本德洛头上，并着手对他重新查访。可没过多久便有消息说，案发当天早晨，有人看见一个男人从范泽伦宅子附近走开，时间恰是凶案必定发生的那阵子。”

===Original===
“Was there anything to connect him with the crime?” Thorndyke asked.
===Chinese===
“有什么东西把那人和凶案连在一起吗？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“Well, there was the time﻿—the small hours of the morning﻿—and the man was carrying a good-sized handbag, which seemed to be pretty heavy and which would have held the stuff that was missing. But the most important point was the man’s appearance. He was described as a smallish man, clean-shaved, with a big hooked nose and very heavy eyebrows set close down over his eyes.
===Chinese===
“唔，时间是其一——清晨那几个钟头；那人还拎着一只不小的手提包，瞧着沉甸甸的，失踪的那些东西也装得下。但最要紧的一条是那人的相貌。据描述，他个子偏矮，脸刮得精光，一只大鹰钩鼻，两道浓眉沉沉压在眼睛上方。

===Original===
“Now this put Bendelow out of it as the principal suspect, because the description didn’t fit him at all” (here I caught Thorndyke’s eye for an instant and was warned afresh, and not unnecessarily, to make no comment); “but,” continued the Superintendent, “it didn’t put him out altogether. For the man whom the description did fit﻿—and it fitted him to a T﻿—was a fellow named Crile﻿—Jonathan Crile﻿—who was a pal of Bendelow’s and was known to have worked with him as a confederate in the receiving business and had been in prison once or twice. So the police started to make inquiries about Crile, and before long they were able to run him to earth. But that didn’t do them much good; for it turned out that Crile wasn’t in New York at all. He was in Philadelphia; and it was clearly proved that he had been there on the day of the murder, on the day before and the day after. So they seemed to have drawn a blank; but they were still a bit suspicious of Mr. Crile, who seems to have been as downy a bird as his friend Bendelow, and of the other chappie, too. But they hadn’t a crumb of evidence against either.
===Chinese===
“这一来，本德洛就从主嫌的位置上摘了出来，因为那副相貌与他全然对不上号”（说到这里，我与桑代克的目光偶然一碰，再度受了警诫——并非多此一举——切莫插嘴）；“可是，”警司继续道，“他也没有就此脱身。因为那副相貌真正合适的人——而且合适得分毫不差——是个名叫克赖尔的家伙——乔纳森·克赖尔——此人是本德洛的伙伴，据知曾同他合伙干销赃的营生，也坐过一两次牢。于是警方着手查访克赖尔，不久便把他的下落挖了出来。可这于事无补；原来克赖尔根本不在纽约，而是在费城；而且确凿查明，案发当日、前一日和后一日，他都在费城。看来这一炮是放空了；但对这位克赖尔先生，他们仍有点嘀咕——此公看来同他的朋友本德洛一样，是只精明狡猾的老鸟——对另一个家伙也如此。可是对这两人，他们连一星半点的证据也没有。

===Original===
“So there the matter stuck. A complete deadlock. There was nothing to be done; for you can’t arrest a man on mere suspicion with not a single fact to support it. But the police kept their eye on both gents, so far as they could, and presently they got a chance. Bendelow made a slip﻿—or, at any rate, they said he did. It was a little trumpery affair, something in the receiving line, and of no importance at all. Probably, a faked charge, too. But they thought that if they could get him arrested they might be able to squeeze something out of him﻿—the police in America can do things that we aren’t allowed to. So they tried to pounce on him. But Mr. Bendelow was a slippery customer, and he got wind of their intentions just in time. When they got into his rooms they found that he had left﻿—in a deuce of a hurry, too, and only a few minutes before they arrived. They searched the place, but found nothing incriminating, and they tried to get on Bendelow’s track, but they didn’t succeed. He had managed to get clear away, and Crile seemed to have disappeared, too.
===Chinese===
“于是事情便僵在了那里，十足的死局。无事可做；单凭嫌疑而无一桩事实撑腰，是不能捕人的。但警方仍尽可能盯住这两位爷，不久便等到了一个机会。本德洛失了一着——至少，他们说他失了一着。那是桩鸡毛蒜皮的破事，销赃一路的勾当，全无分量，多半还是个罗织的罪名。但他们心想，若能把他逮起来，兴许能从他嘴里榨出点东西——美国的警察，有些手段是我们这边不许用的。于是他们打算扑上去拿人。可本德洛先生是块滑不留手的料，恰好及时闻到了风声。他们进得他的住处，发现人已走了——而且走得十万火急，比他们早到不过几分钟。他们搜了那地方，未见任何罪证；想追本德洛的行踪，也没有追上。他脱身逃了个干净利落，克赖尔似乎也一并消失了。

===Original===
“Well, that seemed to be the end of the affair. Both of these crooks had made off without leaving a trace, and the police﻿—having no evidence﻿—didn’t worry any more about them. And so things went on for about a year, until the Van Zellen case had been given up and nearly forgotten. Then something happened quite recently that gave the police a fresh start.
===Chinese===
“得，事情看来就此了结。这两个歹徒跑得无影无踪，警方既然无凭无据，也就不再为他们劳神。如此过了约莫一年光景，范泽伦案已被搁置，几乎淡忘了。恰在最近，出了一件事，给警方添了个新的开头。

===Original===
“It appears that there was a fire in the house in which Bendelow’s rooms were, and a good deal of damage was done, so that they had to do some rebuilding; and in the course of the repairs, the builder’s men found, hidden under the floorboards, a small parcel containing part of the Van Zellen swag. There was nothing of real value; just coins and medals and seal-rings and truck of that kind. But the things were all identified by means of Van Zellen’s catalogue, and, of course, the finding of them in what had been Bendelow’s rooms put the murder pretty clearly on to him.
===Chinese===
“原来，本德洛租住的那个宅子失了一场火，毁损颇重，只得翻修；修缮中间，工人发现地板下面藏着一个小包，里面是范泽伦赃物的一部分。没有什么真正值钱的物件，不过是些钱币、纪念章、图章戒指一类的零碎。可这些东西件件都凭范泽伦的藏品目录对验明白，而且，它们是在本德洛住过的屋子里找到的，这自然把这桩凶案清清楚楚地安到了他头上。

===Original===
“On this, as you can guess, the police and the detective agencies got busy. They searched high and low for the missing man, but for a long time they could pick up no traces of him. At last they discovered that he and Crile had taken a passage nearly a year ago on a tramp steamer bound for England. Thereupon they sent a very smart, experienced detective over to work at the case in conjunction with our own detective department.
===Chinese===
“这一来，您可想而知，警方和各家侦探社都忙开了。他们四处搜捕那个失踪的人，可好长一段时间连一丝踪迹也摸不着。最后总算查明，他和克赖尔约莫一年前订了铺位，乘一艘开往英国的不定期货轮走的。于是他们派了一位十分干练、阅历老到的侦探过来，会同我们这边的侦探部门查办此案。

===Original===
“But we didn’t have much to do with it. The American﻿—Wilson was his name﻿—had all the particulars, with the prison photographs and fingerprints of both the men, and he made most of the inquiries himself. However, there were two things that we did for him. We handed over to him the Van Zellen guinea and the particulars of the D’Arblay murder; and we were able to inform him that his friend, Bendelow, was dead.”
===Chinese===
“不过我们没多少插手的份。那位美国人——名叫威尔逊（Wilson）——手里握着全部详情，连同两人的狱档照片和指纹，大部分查访都是他亲自做的。但有两件事是我们替他办的：我们把那枚范泽伦几尼和达布雷凶案的详情移交给了他；再有，我们能够奉告的是——他的朋友本德洛，死了。”

===Original===
“How did you find that out?” Thorndyke asked.
===Chinese===
“这你们是怎么发现的？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“Oh, quite by chance. One of our men happened to be at Somerset House looking up some details of a will when in the list of wills he came across the name of Simon Bendelow, which he had heard from Wilson himself. He at once got out the will, copied out the address of the executrix and the names and addresses of the witnesses, and handed them over to Wilson, who was mightily taken aback, as you may suppose. However, he wasn’t taking anything for granted. He set off instantly to look up the executrix﻿—a Mrs. Morris. But there he got another disappointment, for the Morrises had gone away and no one knew where they had gone.”
===Chinese===
“哦，纯属偶然。我们一位弟兄恰好在萨默塞特宫（Somerset House）查一份遗嘱的若干细节，在遗嘱清单里撞见了西蒙·本德洛的名字——这名字他听威尔逊亲口提过。他当即调出遗嘱，抄下女执行人的地址和见证人的姓名住址，一并交给了威尔逊。您可想而知，威尔逊大为错愕。不过他什么都不肯想当然，即刻动身去寻那位女执行人——一位莫里斯太太。可在那儿他又碰了一鼻子灰：莫里斯一家已经搬走，谁也不知去向。”

===Original===
“I take it,” said Thorndyke, “that probate of the will had been granted.”
===Chinese===
“是的；那一类手续都已办结。于是威尔逊转而去寻那两位见证人，这回运气好些。那是两位上了年纪的老小姐，同住在霍恩西特恩派克巷（Turnpike Lane）的一所宅子里。她们对本德洛所知不多，因为是在他卧床之后才结识的他。引荐的是他的朋友兼房东太太莫里斯太太，她常领她们上楼到他房里，陪他说说话、宽宽心。不过对于他的死，她们知之甚详：她们见过棺中的他，还一路把他送到了伊尔福德火葬场（Ilford Crematorium）。”

===Original===
“Yes; everything in that way had been finished up. Well, on this, Wilson went off in search of the witnesses, and he had better luck this time. They were two elderly spinsters who lived together in a house in Turnpike Lane, Hornsey. They didn’t know much about Bendelow, for they had only made his acquaintance after he had taken to his bed. They were introduced to him by his friend and landlady, Mrs. Morris, who used to take them up to his room to talk to him and cheer him up a bit. However, they knew all about his death, for they had seen him in his coffin and they followed him to the Ilford Crematorium.”
===Chinese===
“我料想，”桑代克说，“遗嘱认证已经核准了。”

===Original===
“Ha!” said Thorndyke. “So he was cremated.”
===Chinese===
“哈！”桑代克说，“原来他是火化的。”

===Original===
“Yes,” chuckled the Superintendent, with a sly look at Thorndyke. “I thought that would make you prick up your ears, Doctor. Yes, there were no half measures for Mr. Bendelow. He had gone literally to ashes. But it was all right, you know. There couldn’t have been any hanky panky. These two ladies had not only seen him in his coffin; they actually had a last look at him through a little celluloid window in the coffin-lid, just before the coffin was passed through into the cremation furnace.”
===Chinese===
“正是，”警司轻笑着，朝桑代克递去一个狡黠的眼色。“我就知道这话准叫您竖起耳朵，博士。是啊，本德洛先生行事，从来不留半手：他是结结实实化为灰烬了。可您知道，这样反倒安稳，猫腻是万万不会有的。这两位女士不但见过棺中的他；棺材送进火化炉之前，她们还隔着棺盖上那扇小小的赛璐珞窗，最后看了他一眼呢。”

===Original===
“And there was no doubt as to his identity?”
===Chinese===
“他的身份没有疑问吧？”

===Original===
“None whatever. Wilson showed the old ladies his photograph, and they recognized him instantly; picked his photograph out of a dozen others.”
===Chinese===
“毫无疑义。威尔逊把照片拿给两位老太太看，她们当即认出了他——从一打照片里把他的那张挑了出来。”

===Original===
“Where was Bendelow living when they made his acquaintance?”
===Chinese===
“她们与他结识的时候，本德洛住在哪儿？”

===Original===
“Not far from their house; in Abbey Road, Hornsey. But the Morrises moved afterwards to Market Street, Hoxton, and that is where he died and where the will was signed.”
===Chinese===
“离她们家不远，在霍恩西的艾比路。不过莫里斯一家后来搬到了霍克斯顿的市场街，他就是在那儿死的，遗嘱也是在那儿签的。”

===Original===
“I suppose Wilson ascertained the cause of death?”
===Chinese===
“死因我想威尔逊是查证过了的？”

===Original===
“Oh, yes. The old ladies told him that. But he went to Somerset House and got a copy of the death certificate. I haven’t got that, as he took it back with him; but the cause of death was cancer of the pylorus﻿—that’s some part of the gizzard, I believe, but you’ll know all about it. At any rate, there was no doubt on the subject, as the two doctors made a postmortem before they signed the death certificate. It was all perfectly plain and straightforward.
===Chinese===
“哦，查了。两位老太太对他讲了。不过他还是去了萨默塞特宫，调了死亡证明书的副本。这份东西不在我手里，他带回去了；死因是幽门癌——我想那是胃上的什么部位吧，这些您自然门儿清。反正这一节毫无疑义，因为两位医生签署死亡证明书之前，做过尸体剖检。一切明明白白、规规矩矩。”

===Original===
“Well, so much for Mr. Bendelow. When Wilson had done with him, he turned his attention to Crile. And then he really did get a proper shakeup. When he was at Somerset House, looking up Bendelow’s death certificate, it occurred to him just to run his eye down the list and make sure that Crile was still in the land of the living. And there, to his astonishment, he found Crile’s name. He was dead, too! And not only was he dead: he, also, had died of cancer﻿—it was the pancreas this time; another part of the gizzard﻿—and he had died at Hoxton, too, and he had died just four days before Bendelow. The thing was ridiculous. It looked like a conspiracy. But here again everything was plain and aboveboard. Wilson got a copy of the certificate and called on the doctor who had signed it, a man named Usher. Of course, Dr. Usher remembered all about the case as it had occurred quite recently. There was not a shadow of doubt that Crile was dead. Usher had helped to put him in his coffin and had attended at his funeral; and he, too, had no difficulty in picking out Crile’s photograph, and he had no doubt at all as to what Crile died of. So there it was. Queer as it looked, there was no denying the plain facts. Those two crooks had slipped through the fingers of the law, so far as it was possible to see.
===Chinese===
“得，本德洛先生的事便是这些。威尔逊把他办结之后，便把心思转到了克赖尔身上。这一回，他可是结结实实吓了一跳。原来他在萨默塞特宫查本德洛的死亡证明书时，忽然想到顺着名单扫上一眼，好确证克赖尔还在人世。岂料一扫之下，他惊呆了：克赖尔的名字赫然在列。他也死了！而且不光是死：他也死于癌症——这回是胰腺，胃上的又一处——而且也死在霍克斯顿，还恰恰比本德洛早死四天。这事简直荒唐，瞧着倒像一场串通。可到了这一步，一切又都是明白敞亮的。威尔逊调了证明书副本，去拜访签署它的医生——一个名叫厄舍的人。自然，厄舍博士对这桩案子记得清清楚楚，因为事情就出在不久之前。克赖尔死了，这一点没有一丝可疑：厄舍亲手帮着把他装殓入棺，还亲临了葬礼；他也能毫不费力地从照片中挑出克赖尔，而对克赖尔死于何症，更是没有半点疑问。喏，就是这样。瞧着再蹊跷，明白的事实也无从抵赖。这两个歹徒，就眼面前所能看到的而言，已经从法网的指缝里溜了过去。”

===Original===
“But I must admit that I was not quite satisfied; the circumstances were so remarkably odd. I told Wilson so, and I advised him to look further into the matter. I reminded him of the D’Arblay murder and the finding of that guinea, but he said that the murder was our affair, that the men he had come to look for were dead, and that was all that concerned him. So back he went to New York, taking with him the death certificates and the two photographs with the certificates of recognition on the backs of them. But he left the notes of the case with me, on the chance that they might be useful to me, and the two sets of fingerprints, which certainly don’t seem likely to be of much use under the circumstances.”
===Chinese===
“不过我得承认，我心里并不十分踏实；这些情况实在离奇得出奇。我这样对威尔逊说了，还劝他把此事再往深处查一查。我提醒他达布雷凶案和那枚几尼的发现，可他说，那桩凶案是我们的事，他要找的人都已经死了，他所关心的仅此而已。于是他回了纽约，随身带走那些死亡证明书，还有那两张照片——背面都签有辨认证明。但案卷的笔录他留给了我，说不得日后于我有用；还有那两套指纹——照眼下的情形，这东西看来委实派不上什么大用场。”

===Original===
“You never know,” said Thorndyke, with an enigmatical smile.
===Chinese===
“这可说不准，”桑代克说着，脸上浮起一抹高深莫测的微笑。

===Original===
The Superintendent gave him a quick, inquisitive look and agreed: “No, you don’t, especially when you are dealing with Dr. John Thorndyke.” He pulled out his watch, and, staring at it anxiously, exclaimed: “What a confounded nuisance! I’ve got an appointment at the Law Courts in five minutes. It is quite a small matter. Won’t take me more than half an hour. May I come back when I have finished? I should like to hear what you think of this extraordinary story.”
===Chinese===
警司飞快地、探询地看了他一眼，随声附和：“是啊，说不准，尤其是在同约翰·桑代克博士打交道的时候。”他掏出怀表，焦灼地盯住表盘，失声道：“真是该死，麻烦了！五分钟后我在法院还有个约会。事情很小，费不了半个钟头。我办完了可以回来么？我很想听听您对这段离奇故事作何感想。”

===Original===
“Come back, by all means,” said Thorndyke, “and I will turn over the facts in my mind while you are gone. Possibly some suggestion may present itself in the interval.”
===Chinese===
“务必回来，”桑代克说，“趁你不在，我把这些事实在心里掂量掂量。说不定这段时间里会自己冒出个想法来。”

===Original===
He let the officer out, and when the hurried footsteps had died away on the stairs he closed the door and turned to me with a smile.
===Chinese===
他送警官出去，待匆匆的脚步声在楼梯上消逝，他关上门，含笑转过身来望着我。

===Original===
“Well, Gray,” he said, “what do you think of that? Isn’t it a very pretty puzzle for a medical jurist?”
===Chinese===
“怎么样，格雷，”他说，“你对此有何高见？对一位法医学家来说，这岂不是一道绝妙的谜题？”

===Original===
“It is a hopeless tangle to me,” I replied. “My brain is in a whirl. You can’t dispute the facts, and yet you can’t believe them. I don’t know what to make of the affair.”
===Chinese===
“在我看来，这是一团解不开的乱麻，”我答道。“我的脑子乱作一团。事实无可辩驳，却又难以置信。这事我不知该作何解。”

===Original===
“You note the fact that, whoever may be dead, there is somebody alive﻿—very much alive, and that that somebody is the murderer of Julius D’Arblay.”
===Chinese===
“你注意到这个事实：不管死掉的是谁，总还有一个活人——活得好好的——而那个活人，就是尤利乌斯·达布雷的凶手。”

===Original===
“Yes, I realize that. But obviously he can’t be either Crile or Bendelow. The question is: Who is he?”
===Chinese===
“这我明白。可他显然不可能是克赖尔，也不可能是本德洛。问题是：他是谁？”

===Original===
“You note the link between him and the Van Zellen murder; I mean the electrotype guinea?”
===Chinese===
“你注意到他与范泽伦凶案之间的关联了吧——我指的就是那枚电铸几尼？”

===Original===
“Yes, there is evidently some connection, but I can’t imagine what it can be. By the way, you noticed that the American police had got muddled about the personal appearance of these two men. The description of that man who was seen coming away from Van Zellen’s house, and who was said to be quite unlike Bendelow, actually fitted him perfectly. They had evidently made a mistake of some kind.”
===Chinese===
“注意到了，显然有某种牵连，可我想象不出是什么。顺便说一句，你留意到没有，美国警方把这两人的相貌特征弄拧了。那个被人看见从范泽伦宅子走开、据说与本德洛毫不相似的男人，那副相貌其实恰恰与他不差分毫。他们分明是出了什么岔子。”

===Original===
“Yes, I noticed that. But the description may have fitted Crile better. We must get into touch with this man, Usher. I wonder if he will be the Usher who used to attend at St. Margaret’s.”
===Chinese===
“我留意到了。不过那副相貌或许更契合克赖尔。我们必须同厄舍这个人接上头。不知他会不会就是从前常在圣玛格丽特医院走动的那个厄舍。”

===Original===
“He is; and I am in touch with him already. In fact, he was telling me about this very patient, Jonathan Crile.”
===Chinese===
“正是他；而且我已经同他接上了头。说起来，他正同我讲过这位病人——乔纳森·克赖尔呢。”

===Original===
“Indeed! Can you remember the substance of what he told you?”
===Chinese===
“是么！他说的话，你还记得个大意么？”

===Original===
“I think so. It wasn’t very thrilling.” And here I gave him, as well as I could remember them, the details with which Usher had entertained me of his attendance on the late Jonathan Crile, his dealings with the landlady, Mrs. Pepper, and the incidents of the funeral, including Usher’s triumphant return in the mourning coach. It seemed a dull and trivial story, but Thorndyke listened to it with the keenest interest, and when I had finished he asked: “He didn’t happen to mention where Crile lived, I suppose?”
===Chinese===
“记得，我想记得。那倒算不上多么惊险。”于是我把厄舍讲给我听的那些细节，凭记忆所能及，一一复述给他：他如何诊治已故的乔纳森·克赖尔，如何同房东太太佩珀太太打交道，以及葬礼上的种种情事——包括他坐着送葬马车凯旋而归。这故事听着枯燥琐屑，桑代克却听得兴味十足；待我讲完，他问道：“他想必没有顺带提起克赖尔住在哪儿吧？”

===Original===
“Yes, curiously enough, he did. The address, I remember, was 52, Field Street, Hoxton.”
===Chinese===
“提起了，说来也巧。那地址我记得，是霍克斯顿菲尔德街 52 号。”

===Original===
“Ha!” said Thorndyke. “You are a mine of information, Gray.”
===Chinese===
“哈！”桑代克说。“你可真是一座情报宝库，格雷。”

===Original===
He rose and, taking down from the bookshelves Phillip’s Atlas of London, opened it and pored over one of the maps. Then, replacing the Atlas, he got out his notes of the D’Arblay case and searched for a particular entry. It was evidently quite a short one, for when he had found it he gave it but a single glance and closed the portfolio. Then, returning to the bookshelves, he took out the Post Office Directory and opened it at the “streets” section. Here also his search was but a short one though it appeared to be concerned with two separate items; for, having examined one, he turned to a different part of the section to find the other. Finally he closed the unwieldy volume and, having replaced it on the shelf, turned and once more looked at me inquiringly.
===Chinese===
他站起身，从书架上取下《菲利普伦敦地图集》（Phillip's Atlas of London），翻开其中一幅地图细细研看。然后他把地图集放回原处，取出他那卷达布雷案笔记，翻寻一条特定的记录。那条记录显然很短，找到之后，他只扫了一眼，便合上了卷宗夹。接着他回到书架前，抽出那部邮政人名地址录，翻到“街名”部分。这一回的查找为时也不长，只是似乎涉及两个分开的条目：查完一个，他又翻到该部分的另一处去找另一个。最后他合上那部笨重的大书，放回架上，转过身来，又用探询的目光望着我。

===Original===
“Reflecting on what Miller has told us,” he said, “does anything suggest itself to you? Any sort of hypothesis as to what the real facts may be?”
===Chinese===
“把米勒方才所讲的话琢磨一遍，”他说，“你心里可冒出什么来？关于事情真相究竟如何，可有半点假说？”

===Original===
“Nothing whatever,” I replied. “The confusion that was already in my mind is only the worse confounded. But that is not your case, I take it.”
===Chinese===
“半点也没有，”我答道。“我脑子里原有的那团乱，如今只是乱上加乱。不过我想，你可不是这样。”

===Original===
“Not entirely,” he admitted. “The fact is that I had already formed a hypothesis as to the motives and circumstances which lay behind the murder of Julius D’Arblay, and I find this new matter not inconsistent with it. But that hypothesis may, nevertheless, turn out to be quite wrong when we put it to the test of further investigation.”
===Chinese===
“不尽然，”他承认道。“实情是，对于尤利乌斯·达布雷谋杀案背后的动机与情由，我早已形成了一个假说，而这段新情况与它也并不相悖。不过，一经进一步调查的检验，这假说也未必不会错个彻底。”

===Original===
“You have some further investigation in view, then.”
===Chinese===
“这么说，你已有了进一步调查的打算。”

===Original===
“Yes. I am going to make a proposal to Superintendent Miller﻿—and here he comes, before his time; by which I judge that he, also, is keen on the solution of this puzzle.”
===Chinese===
“是的。我正要向米勒警司提一项建议——喏，他来了，还提前了；由此我断定，他自己也一心想解这道谜呢。”

===Original===
Thorndyke’s opinion seemed to be justified, for the Superintendent entered all agog, and opened the subject at once.
===Chinese===
桑代克的判断看来不错，因为警司进门时一副急不可耐的神气，而且开门见山，随即入了正题。

===Original===
“Well, Doctor, I suppose you have been thinking over Wilson’s story? How does it strike you? Have you come to any conclusion?”
===Chinese===
“怎么样，博士，我想您把威尔逊的故事琢磨过了吧？您觉得如何？得出什么结论没有？”

===Original===
“Yes,” replied Thorndyke. “I have come to the conclusion that I can’t accept that story at its face value as representing the actual facts.”
===Chinese===
“得出了，”桑代克答道。“我的结论是：这个故事，我不能照它表面的样子当作实情接受下来。”

===Original===
Miller laughed with an air of mingled amusement and vexation. “That is just my position,” said he. “The story seems incredible, but yet you can’t raise any objection. The evidence in support of it is absolutely conclusive at every point. There isn’t a single weak spot in it﻿—at least, I haven’t found one. Perhaps you have?” And here he looked at Thorndyke with eager inquiry in his eyes.
===Chinese===
米勒笑了，神情里好笑和懊恼参半。“这正是我的处境，”他说。“这故事听着难以置信，可你又提不出异议。支撑它的证据在每一点上都铁板钉钉，没有一处破绽——至少，我没有找到。也许您找到了？”说到这里，他望着桑代克，眼里满是热切的探询。

===Original===
“I won’t say that,” Thorndyke replied. “But I put it to you, Miller, that the alleged facts that are offered are too abnormal to be entertained. We cannot accept that string of coincidences. It must be obvious to you that there is a fallacy somewhere and that the actual facts are not what they seem.”
===Chinese===
“那倒不能说，”桑代克答道。“可是我要向你进一言，米勒：摆在我们面前的这些所谓事实，悖谬得太不寻常，令人无从接受。那一连串的巧合，我们接受不了。你必定看得分明：这里头准有个症结，实情与表象并不一致。”

===Original===
“Yes, I feel that, myself,” rejoined Miller. “But what are we to do? How are we to find the flaw in the evidence, if there is one? Can you see where to look for it? I believe you can.”
===Chinese===
“这我自己也感觉得到，”米勒应道。“可我们怎么办？倘若证据里真有漏洞，我们又该怎样把它找出来？您看得出该往哪里找么？我相信您看得出来。”

===Original===
“I think there is one point which ought to be verified,” said Thorndyke. “The identification of Crile doesn’t strike me as perfectly convincing.”
===Chinese===
“我以为有一点应当加以核实，”桑代克说。“克赖尔的身份认定，在我看来算不得无懈可击。”

===Original===
“How does his case differ from Bendelow’s?” Miller demanded.
===Chinese===
“他这案子同本德洛的有什么两样？”米勒诘问道。

===Original===
“In two respects,” was the reply. “First, Bendelow was identified by two persons who had known him well for some time and who gave a most circumstantial account of his illness, his death, and the disposal of his body; and second, Bendelow’s remains have been cremated and are therefore, presumably, beyond our reach for purposes of identification.”
===Chinese===
“有两样不同，”回答是这样的。“其一，认定本德洛的两个人与他相熟已久，而且对他的病、他的死、他遗体的处置，都作了极详尽的叙述；其二，本德洛的遗体已经火化，因此，就身份认定而言，想必已非我们力所能及。”

===Original===
“Well,” Miller objected, “Crile isn’t so very accessible, being some few feet under ground.”
===Chinese===
“哦，”米勒提出异议，“克赖尔也没好到哪儿去，人家在地下埋着好几英尺呢。”

===Original===
“Still, he is there; and he has been buried only a few weeks. It would be possible to exhume the body and settle the question of his identity once for all.”
===Chinese===
“话虽如此，他总归在那里；而且入土不过几个星期。把尸体起出来，一劳永逸地了断他的身份问题，是办得到的。”

===Original===
“Then you are not satisfied with Dr. Usher’s identification?”
===Chinese===
“这么说，您对厄舍博士的认定不满意？”

===Original===
“No. Usher saw him only after a long, wasting illness, which must have altered his appearance very greatly; whereas the photograph was taken when Crile was in his normal health. It couldn’t have been so very like Usher’s patient.”
===Chinese===
“不满意。厄舍见到他时，他已缠绵多日、形容枯槁——那样的久病必定使容貌大变；而那张照片摄于克赖尔康健之时。它同厄舍的那位病人，未必有几分相像。”

===Original===
“That’s true,” said Miller; “and I remember that Usher wasn’t so very positive, according to Wilson. But he agreed that it seemed to be the same man; and all the other facts seemed to point to the certainty that it was really Crile. Still, you are not satisfied? It’s a pity Wilson took the photograph back with him.”
===Chinese===
“这话不假，”米勒说；“而且我记得，据威尔逊说，厄舍当时也不怎么斩钉截铁。不过他承认，看来是同一个人；其余种种事实，似乎也都确凿指向那正是克赖尔。可您还是不满意？可惜威尔逊把照片带回去了。”

===Original===
“The photograph is of no consequence,” said Thorndyke. “You have the fingerprints; properly authenticated fingerprints, actually taken from the man in the presence of witnesses. After this short time it will be possible to get perfectly recognizable fingerprints from the body, and those fingerprints will settle the identity of Usher’s patient beyond any possible doubt.”
===Chinese===
“照片无关紧要，”桑代克说。“您手里有指纹——手续完备、当着见证人之面从本人身上采取的指纹。时隔不久，从尸体上取得清清楚楚、可以辨认的指纹，是办得到的；而那些指纹，将把厄舍那位病人的身份，定得再无半点疑问。”

===Original===
The Superintendent scratched his chin thoughtfully. “It’s a bit of a job to get an exhumation order,” said he. “Before I raise the question with the Commissioner, I should like to have a rather more definite opinion from you. Do you seriously doubt that the man in that coffin is Jonathan Crile?”
===Chinese===
警司若有所思地挠着下巴。“弄一纸掘尸令，可是件费周折的事，”他说。“在向总监（Commissioner）提起之前，我想先从您这里讨一个更明确些的意见。您当真怀疑，棺中那人不是乔纳森·克赖尔？”

===Original===
“It is my opinion,” replied Thorndyke﻿—“of course, I may be wrong﻿—but it is my considered opinion that the Crile who is in that coffin is not the Crile whose fingerprints are in your possession.”
===Chinese===
“依我之见——当然，我也许错了——但这是我深思熟虑后的意见：棺中那个克赖尔，并非您手中握有指纹的那个克赖尔。”

===Original===
“Very well, Doctor,” said Miller, rising and picking up his hat, “that is good enough for me. I won’t ask you for your reasons because I know you won’t give them. But I have known you long enough to feel sure that you wouldn’t give a definite opinion like that unless you had got something pretty solid to go on. And I don’t think we shall have any difficulty about the exhumation order after what you have said.”
===Chinese===
“很好，博士，”米勒说着，站起身拿起帽子，“这对我已经足够了。我不问您的理由，因为我知道您不会说。但同您相识这么久，我确信：您若没有相当扎实的依据，绝不会给出这样明确的意见。而且听您这一席话，我想那纸掘尸令，我们是不难到手的。”

===Original===
With this the Superintendent took his leave, and very shortly afterwards Thorndyke carried me off to lunch at his club before dismissing me to take up my duties at the studio.
===Chinese===
说完，警司便告辞了。没过多久，桑代克就把我带去他的俱乐部用了午餐，随后打发我去画室履职。
